# 19. Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge

> **Track:** GPU and Accelerator Management for Red Hat OpenShift AI
> **Baseline:** OpenShift Container Platform 4.21 and Red Hat OpenShift AI Self-Managed 3.5
> **Audience:** Corporate/L3 platform engineers, OpenShift administrators, MLOps engineers, SREs, and senior support engineers
> **Primary lab outcome:** Inventory legacy AcceleratorProfile objects, map them to hardware profiles, test application behavior, and produce a reversible migration plan.
> **Generated:** 2026-07-21

## Version and support notice

This chapter is a production-oriented learning guide, not a substitute for the product support matrix. Before implementation, verify the exact OpenShift, OpenShift AI, operator, firmware, driver, runtime image, accelerator model, hypervisor, and architecture combination in the current Red Hat and vendor documentation. Commands containing angle-bracket values are templates and must be adapted to the target cluster.

Current OpenShift AI releases use **hardware profiles** for governed resource selection. The separate chapter on accelerator profiles preserves legacy knowledge for upgrades and older environments. IBM Spyre, AMD, Intel Gaudi, vGPU, and multi-node designs have platform- and release-specific boundaries; never infer support from generic Kubernetes functionality alone.

## Executive summary

Retain operational knowledge of the earlier accelerator-profile workflow for upgrades, migrations, must-gather analysis, and support of older OpenShift AI environments while preferring hardware profiles for current deployments.

The central operational object in this chapter is `AcceleratorProfile custom resource`. The primary controller or integration is **Red Hat OpenShift AI Operator**, and the representative namespace or scope is `OpenShift AI control plane`.

- Start at the physical and firmware layer before assuming Kubernetes is at fault.
- Separate discovery, driver, runtime, scheduling, and application layers.
- Use node capacity and allocatable fields as the scheduler source of truth.
- Use pod events before reading long application logs.
- Correlate changes with MachineConfigPool, kernel, operator, and image updates.
- Preserve evidence before rebooting or deleting failed operands.
- Validate remediation with a minimal vendor smoke workload and the real application.
- Prefer reversible GitOps changes with explicit rollback steps.
- Measure queue time, utilization, memory headroom, and failure rate together.
- Treat accelerator support matrices as release-specific contracts.

## Learning objectives

1. Explain Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge to an architecture review board without relying on dashboard screenshots.
2. Identify every control-plane and node-level component involved in Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge.
3. Create the inventory legacy acceleratorprofile objects, map them to hardware profiles, test application behavior, and produce a reversible migration plan.
4. Validate `AcceleratorProfile custom resource` from discovery through workload execution.
5. Differentiate a hardware problem from a driver, device-plugin, runtime, scheduler, quota, storage, network, or application problem.
6. Design change, rollback, maintenance, monitoring, and escalation procedures.
7. Apply least privilege, tenant isolation, capacity governance, and audit controls.
8. Create evidence suitable for an L3 support case without destroying the original failure state.
9. Translate dashboard symptoms into oc, node, kernel, runtime, and application-level checks.
10. Explain version-sensitive and support-sensitive decisions explicitly.

## Architecture and control flow

```text
[Physical accelerator / firmware / PCIe or virtual device]
                         |
                         v
[Node OS and kernel] -> [Vendor driver or guest driver]
                         |
                         v
[NFD / feature discovery] ----> [Node labels and policy facts]
                         |
                         v
[Vendor device plugin] --------> [Node capacity and allocatable: AcceleratorProfile custom resource]
                         |
                         v
[OpenShift scheduler + quota + affinity + taints]
                         |
                         v
[CRI-O + vendor container toolkit / CDI / OCI hooks]
                         |
                         v
[Workbench, training pod, KServe runtime, or vLLM workload]
                         |
                         v
[DCGM/vendor telemetry + Prometheus + alerts + SLO dashboards]
```

- The physical or virtual accelerator must first be discoverable by the operating system.
- A compatible kernel driver binds the device and creates the required character devices or vendor interfaces.
- Feature discovery labels facts; labels do not allocate a device by themselves.
- A device plugin registers with kubelet and publishes a vendor-qualified extended resource.
- The scheduler places only a pod whose resource request, affinity, tolerations, and quota can all be satisfied.
- The container runtime injects device nodes and matching user-space libraries or CDI specifications.
- The application framework performs a final enumeration and initialization step.
- Telemetry must observe both infrastructure health and workload-level service indicators.

### Capacity versus allocatable

Capacity represents discovered resources; allocatable is the scheduler-usable quantity after kubelet and health decisions.

### Extended resource semantics

Accelerators are vendor-qualified integer resources and are not CPU-style overcommittable resources.

## Prerequisite and readiness matrix

| Layer | Required evidence | Pass condition | Typical owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hardware | Supported accelerator model, firmware, power, cooling, PCIe topology | Vendor inventory and diagnostics pass | Infrastructure |
| Platform | Supported OpenShift and OpenShift AI versions | Compatibility matrix verified | Platform engineering |
| Node OS | RHCOS/kernel level and MachineConfigPool health | MCP Updated=True and Degraded=False | OpenShift admin |
| Discovery | NFD and vendor labels | Expected nodes have stable labels | Platform engineering |
| Driver | Kernel module, version, device nodes | Driver loads and vendor CLI works | Accelerator admin |
| Device plugin | `AcceleratorProfile custom resource` capacity and allocatable | Expected integer resource is advertised | OpenShift admin |
| Runtime | CRI-O and vendor toolkit or CDI | Container can enumerate assigned device | Platform engineering |
| Scheduling | Labels, taints, affinity, quota | Test pod is admitted and scheduled | Platform engineering |
| Application | Supported framework and image | Smoke workload completes | ML engineering |
| Monitoring | Metrics, alerts, dashboard, runbook | Telemetry and test alert verified | SRE |

## Project from scratch

**Project goal:** Inventory legacy AcceleratorProfile objects, map them to hardware profiles, test application behavior, and produce a reversible migration plan.

### Phase 0 - Architecture decision record

1. Record business workload, model or training framework, target SLO, concurrency, data sensitivity, and expected growth.
2. Identify supported accelerator models and the exact platform architecture.
3. Choose exclusive, shared, MIG, vGPU, passthrough, or vendor-native allocation only after isolation and support review.
4. Define failure domain, maintenance window, rollback trigger, capacity reserve, and ownership.

### Phase 1 - Cluster and node preparation

1. Verify cluster operators and MachineConfigPools are healthy before introducing accelerator changes.
2. Confirm firmware, IOMMU, Secure Boot, PCIe, NUMA, and hypervisor settings as applicable.
3. Create or identify a dedicated worker pool and approve labels and taints through GitOps.
4. Capture a before-state inventory for nodes, kernel, PCI devices, and installed operators.

### Phase 2 - Operator and operand installation

1. Install and verify Red Hat OpenShift AI Operator in the approved namespace and channel.
2. Create the required custom resource only from current vendor or Red Hat examples.
3. Observe daemonsets, deployments, jobs, validation pods, events, and ClusterServiceVersion conditions.
4. Do not proceed until desired, current, ready, and available operand counts agree.

### Phase 3 - Resource publication and governance

1. Verify node capacity and allocatable include `AcceleratorProfile custom resource` or the release-specific equivalent.
2. Create stable policy labels separately from automatically generated discovery labels.
3. Apply taints, tolerations, namespace quota, and hardware profiles.
4. Validate RBAC so ordinary users cannot change cluster-scoped accelerator configuration.

### Phase 4 - Minimal smoke workload

1. Run the smallest vendor-supported image and discovery command before testing a large model.
2. Confirm the pod was assigned to the expected node and received exactly the requested device count.
3. Collect the vendor CLI output and framework enumeration output from inside the container.
4. Delete the pod and verify the resource returns to allocatable capacity.

### Phase 5 - OpenShift AI workload

1. Create or select a hardware profile with the required resource identifier and scheduling policy.
2. Create a GPU-enabled workbench or model deployment with a supported image and storage path.
3. Validate readiness, logs, endpoint behavior, performance, and resource release during shutdown.
4. Run a representative workload rather than relying only on a synthetic discovery command.

### Phase 6 - Monitoring and production acceptance

1. Expose vendor telemetry to Prometheus using supported ServiceMonitor integration.
2. Create alerts for device loss, memory pressure, sustained thermal issues, Xid or vendor errors, and long Pending duration.
3. Define operational SLOs for queue time, workload readiness, inference latency, throughput, and accelerator health.
4. Complete rollback, backup, must-gather, and escalation rehearsals before production sign-off.


## Reference lab manifest

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Namespace
metadata:
  name: OpenShift AI control plane
  labels:
    purpose: gpu-accelerator-l3-lab
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: ResourceQuota
metadata:
  name: accelerator-quota
  namespace: OpenShift AI control plane
spec:
  hard:
    requests.nvidia.com/gpu: "2"  # Replace for AMD, Gaudi, Spyre, or MIG resources.
    requests.cpu: "16"
    requests.memory: 64Gi
    limits.cpu: "32"
    limits.memory: 128Gi
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: accelerator-smoke-test
  namespace: OpenShift AI control plane
  labels:
    app: accelerator-smoke-test
spec:
  restartPolicy: Never
  nodeSelector:
    accelerator.platform.example.com/enabled: "true"  # Replace with approved label.
  tolerations:
  - key: accelerator.platform.example.com/dedicated
    operator: Equal
    value: "true"
    effect: NoSchedule
  containers:
  - name: test
    image: <approved-vendor-runtime-image>
    imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent
    command: ["/bin/bash", "-lc"]
    args:
    - |
      set -euxo pipefail
      echo "Run vendor-specific discovery here"
      sleep 30
    resources:
      requests:
        cpu: "1"
        memory: 2Gi
        nvidia.com/gpu: "1"
      limits:
        cpu: "2"
        memory: 4Gi
        nvidia.com/gpu: "1"
```

### Apply and validate

```bash
oc apply -f accelerator-lab.yaml
```

```bash
oc get resourcequota -n OpenShift AI control plane
```

```bash
oc describe pod accelerator-smoke-test -n gpu-lab
```

```bash
oc get pod accelerator-smoke-test -n gpu-lab -o wide
```

```bash
oc describe node <assigned-node> | sed -n '/Capacity:/,/System Info:/p'
```

## Security, governance, and compliance

### Control 01: Use vendor-supported operators and signed images from approved registries.

- **Why it matters for Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge:** accelerator components commonly require elevated node access, and a configuration error can affect every workload on the node.
- **Implementation evidence:** Git commit, approved change record, RBAC export, operator custom resource, policy result, and validation output.
- **Failure signal:** unexpected privileged workload, unmanaged image, manual node drift, resource bypass, or missing audit trail.
- **Review cadence:** before release, after upgrade, after incident, and at least quarterly for production clusters.

### Control 02: Restrict privileged operand permissions to the operator-managed namespaces.

- **Why it matters for Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge:** accelerator components commonly require elevated node access, and a configuration error can affect every workload on the node.
- **Implementation evidence:** Git commit, approved change record, RBAC export, operator custom resource, policy result, and validation output.
- **Failure signal:** unexpected privileged workload, unmanaged image, manual node drift, resource bypass, or missing audit trail.
- **Review cadence:** before release, after upgrade, after incident, and at least quarterly for production clusters.

### Control 03: Protect GPU worker nodes with taints and explicit tolerations.

- **Why it matters for Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge:** accelerator components commonly require elevated node access, and a configuration error can affect every workload on the node.
- **Implementation evidence:** Git commit, approved change record, RBAC export, operator custom resource, policy result, and validation output.
- **Failure signal:** unexpected privileged workload, unmanaged image, manual node drift, resource bypass, or missing audit trail.
- **Review cadence:** before release, after upgrade, after incident, and at least quarterly for production clusters.

### Control 04: Use namespace quotas for extended accelerator resources.

- **Why it matters for Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge:** accelerator components commonly require elevated node access, and a configuration error can affect every workload on the node.
- **Implementation evidence:** Git commit, approved change record, RBAC export, operator custom resource, policy result, and validation output.
- **Failure signal:** unexpected privileged workload, unmanaged image, manual node drift, resource bypass, or missing audit trail.
- **Review cadence:** before release, after upgrade, after incident, and at least quarterly for production clusters.

### Control 05: Apply least-privilege RBAC for hardware profile administration.

- **Why it matters for Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge:** accelerator components commonly require elevated node access, and a configuration error can affect every workload on the node.

- Capacity checkpoint 001: correlate **legacy profile count** with workload class `1`, concurrency tier `1`, and failure reserve `15%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.
- Capacity checkpoint 002: correlate **migration completion** with workload class `2`, concurrency tier `2`, and failure reserve `20%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.
- Capacity checkpoint 003: correlate **failed profile mappings** with workload class `3`, concurrency tier `3`, and failure reserve `25%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.
- Capacity checkpoint 004: correlate **user impact incidents** with workload class `4`, concurrency tier `4`, and failure reserve `30%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.
- Capacity checkpoint 005: correlate **rollback readiness** with workload class `5`, concurrency tier `5`, and failure reserve `35%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.
- Capacity checkpoint 006: correlate **stale CR count** with workload class `1`, concurrency tier `6`, and failure reserve `10%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.
- Capacity checkpoint 007: correlate **legacy profile count** with workload class `2`, concurrency tier `7`, and failure reserve `15%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.
- Capacity checkpoint 008: correlate **migration completion** with workload class `3`, concurrency tier `8`, and failure reserve `20%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.
- Capacity checkpoint 009: correlate **failed profile mappings** with workload class `4`, concurrency tier `1`, and failure reserve `25%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.
- Capacity checkpoint 010: correlate **user impact incidents** with workload class `5`, concurrency tier `2`, and failure reserve `30%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.
- Capacity checkpoint 011: correlate **rollback readiness** with workload class `1`, concurrency tier `3`, and failure reserve `35%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.
- Capacity checkpoint 012: correlate **stale CR count** with workload class `2`, concurrency tier `4`, and failure reserve `10%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.
- Capacity checkpoint 013: correlate **legacy profile count** with workload class `3`, concurrency tier `5`, and failure reserve `15%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.
- Capacity checkpoint 014: correlate **migration completion** with workload class `4`, concurrency tier `6`, and failure reserve `20%`; record saturation evidence and scaling action.

## Monitoring design

### Core metrics

- legacy profile count
- migration completion
- failed profile mappings
- user impact incidents
- rollback readiness
- stale CR count

```yaml
apiVersion: monitoring.coreos.com/v1
kind: PrometheusRule
metadata:
  name: accelerator-health-rules
  namespace: gpu-lab
spec:
  groups:
  - name: accelerator.rules
    rules:
    - alert: AcceleratorNodeHasNoAllocatableDevice
      expr: kube_node_status_allocatable{resource="AcceleratorProfile_custom_resource"} == 0
      for: 15m
      labels:
        severity: warning
      annotations:
        summary: "Accelerator node reports zero allocatable devices"
        runbook_url: "https://runbooks.example.com/accelerators/no-allocatable-device"
    - alert: AcceleratorPodPendingTooLong
      expr: max_over_time(kube_pod_status_phase{phase="Pending",namespace="gpu-lab"}[30m]) > 0
      for: 20m
      labels:
        severity: warning
      annotations:
        summary: "Accelerator workload has remained Pending"
```

### Dashboard layout

- Fleet accelerator inventory by vendor and product
- Node capacity, allocatable, requested, and active workload count
- GPU or accelerator utilization by node and workload
- Memory used, free, and high-water mark
- Temperature, power, clocks, throttling, and health
- Pending workloads and scheduler reason
- Operator and daemonset readiness
- Driver and runtime version drift
- Xid, ECC, PCIe, reset, and vendor error events
- Inference or training service indicators

## L3 troubleshooting method

```text
1. Scope the blast radius.
2. Identify the first failing layer.
3. Preserve before-state evidence.
4. Check recent changes and version drift.
5. Reproduce with the smallest supported test.
6. Form one falsifiable hypothesis.
7. Run the least disruptive test.
8. Remediate with rollback prepared.
9. Validate platform and real workload.
10. Document prevention and monitoring improvements.
```

### Layer 1: Physical device and firmware

- **Question:** Can this layer independently explain the observed `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` symptom?
- **Evidence:** versions, status, events, logs, node state, resource state, and a minimal test.
- **Do not do first:** reboot, reinstall, delete all operands, or change multiple variables simultaneously.
- **Exit criterion:** either prove the layer healthy or identify a concrete failure and owner.

### Layer 2: Hypervisor or passthrough layer

- **Question:** Can this layer independently explain the observed `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` symptom?
- **Evidence:** versions, status, events, logs, node state, resource state, and a minimal test.
- **Do not do first:** reboot, reinstall, delete all operands, or change multiple variables simultaneously.
- **Exit criterion:** either prove the layer healthy or identify a concrete failure and owner.

## Scenario-based L3 drills

### Scenario 001: profile remains referenced

- **Context:** `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` in workload tier `1` on accelerator pool `gpu-pool-1`.
- **Primary hypothesis:** missing node label or toleration.
- **First evidence:** pod events, node allocatable `AcceleratorProfile custom resource`, operand readiness, recent changes, and `rollback readiness`.
- **Command path:** `oc get nodes -o wide` followed by `chroot /host`.
- **Safe test:** run a minimal supported accelerator container on the same node without changing the production workload.
- **Remediation rule:** change one layer at a time and keep a tested rollback manifest or node-state recovery step.
- **Validation:** prove device visibility, resource allocation, representative workload success, metrics recovery, and no regression on another node.
- **Prevention:** add a preflight check, drift alert, canary node, or admission control matching the discovered cause.
- **Escalation evidence:** must-gather, operator logs, node journal, hardware diagnostics, exact versions, timestamps, and reproducer.

### Scenario 002: migration changes scheduling

- **Context:** `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` in workload tier `2` on accelerator pool `gpu-pool-2`.
- **Primary hypothesis:** device-plugin registration or health issue.
- **First evidence:** pod events, node allocatable `AcceleratorProfile custom resource`, operand readiness, recent changes, and `user impact incidents`.
- **Command path:** `oc get node <gpu-node> -o json | jq '.status.capacity,.status.allocatable'` followed by `lspci -nn | grep -Ei 'vga|3d|display|nvidia|amd|habana|ibm'`.
- **Safe test:** run a minimal supported accelerator container on the same node without changing the production workload.
- **Remediation rule:** change one layer at a time and keep a tested rollback manifest or node-state recovery step.
- **Validation:** prove device visibility, resource allocation, representative workload success, metrics recovery, and no regression on another node.
- **Prevention:** add a preflight check, drift alert, canary node, or admission control matching the discovered cause.
- **Escalation evidence:** must-gather, operator logs, node journal, hardware diagnostics, exact versions, timestamps, and reproducer.

### Scenario 003: display name collision

- **Context:** `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` in workload tier `3` on accelerator pool `gpu-pool-3`.
- **Primary hypothesis:** unsupported hardware, platform, or release combination.
- **First evidence:** pod events, node allocatable `AcceleratorProfile custom resource`, operand readiness, recent changes, and `failed profile mappings`.
- **Command path:** `oc describe node <gpu-node>` followed by `lsmod | grep -Ei 'nvidia|amdgpu|vfio|habana'`.
- **Safe test:** run a minimal supported accelerator container on the same node without changing the production workload.
- **Remediation rule:** change one layer at a time and keep a tested rollback manifest or node-state recovery step.
- **Validation:** prove device visibility, resource allocation, representative workload success, metrics recovery, and no regression on another node.
- **Prevention:** add a preflight check, drift alert, canary node, or admission control matching the discovered cause.
- **Escalation evidence:** must-gather, operator logs, node journal, hardware diagnostics, exact versions, timestamps, and reproducer.

### Scenario 004: resource identifier mismatch

- **Context:** `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` in workload tier `4` on accelerator pool `gpu-pool-4`.
- **Primary hypothesis:** incorrect resource name or profile identifier.
- **First evidence:** pod events, node allocatable `AcceleratorProfile custom resource`, operand readiness, recent changes, and `migration completion`.
- **Command path:** `oc get pods -A -o wide | grep -Ei 'gpu|nvidia|amd|habana|spyre|nfd'` followed by `oc get csv -A`.
- **Safe test:** run a minimal supported accelerator container on the same node without changing the production workload.
- **Remediation rule:** change one layer at a time and keep a tested rollback manifest or node-state recovery step.
- **Validation:** prove device visibility, resource allocation, representative workload success, metrics recovery, and no regression on another node.
- **Prevention:** add a preflight check, drift alert, canary node, or admission control matching the discovered cause.
- **Escalation evidence:** must-gather, operator logs, node journal, hardware diagnostics, exact versions, timestamps, and reproducer.

### Scenario 005: legacy CRD assumptions applied to current release

- **Context:** `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` in workload tier `5` on accelerator pool `gpu-pool-1`.
- **Primary hypothesis:** driver and user-space incompatibility.
- **First evidence:** pod events, node allocatable `AcceleratorProfile custom resource`, operand readiness, recent changes, and `legacy profile count`.
- **Command path:** `oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp | tail -100` followed by `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`.
- **Safe test:** run a minimal supported accelerator container on the same node without changing the production workload.
- **Remediation rule:** change one layer at a time and keep a tested rollback manifest or node-state recovery step.
- **Validation:** prove device visibility, resource allocation, representative workload success, metrics recovery, and no regression on another node.
- **Prevention:** add a preflight check, drift alert, canary node, or admission control matching the discovered cause.
- **Escalation evidence:** must-gather, operator logs, node journal, hardware diagnostics, exact versions, timestamps, and reproducer.

### Scenario 006: works on one GPU node but not another

- **Context:** `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` in workload tier `1` on accelerator pool `gpu-pool-2`.
- **Primary hypothesis:** application memory or topology problem.
- **First evidence:** pod events, node allocatable `AcceleratorProfile custom resource`, operand readiness, recent changes, and `stale CR count`.
- **Command path:** `oc adm node-logs <gpu-node> --path=journal | grep -Ei 'nvidia|amdgpu|habana|xid|vfio|iommu'` followed by `oc get installplans.operators.coreos.com -A`.
- **Safe test:** run a minimal supported accelerator container on the same node without changing the production workload.
- **Remediation rule:** change one layer at a time and keep a tested rollback manifest or node-state recovery step.
- **Validation:** prove device visibility, resource allocation, representative workload success, metrics recovery, and no regression on another node.
- **Prevention:** add a preflight check, drift alert, canary node, or admission control matching the discovered cause.
- **Escalation evidence:** must-gather, operator logs, node journal, hardware diagnostics, exact versions, timestamps, and reproducer.

### Scenario 007: works on host but not in container

- **Context:** `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` in workload tier `2` on accelerator pool `gpu-pool-3`.
- **Primary hypothesis:** version drift between nodes.
- **First evidence:** pod events, node allocatable `AcceleratorProfile custom resource`, operand readiness, recent changes, and `rollback readiness`.
- **Command path:** `oc debug node/<gpu-node>` followed by `oc get mcp`.
- **Safe test:** run a minimal supported accelerator container on the same node without changing the production workload.
- **Remediation rule:** change one layer at a time and keep a tested rollback manifest or node-state recovery step.
- **Validation:** prove device visibility, resource allocation, representative workload success, metrics recovery, and no regression on another node.
- **Prevention:** add a preflight check, drift alert, canary node, or admission control matching the discovered cause.
- **Escalation evidence:** must-gather, operator logs, node journal, hardware diagnostics, exact versions, timestamps, and reproducer.

- **Context:** `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` in workload tier `3` on accelerator pool `gpu-pool-4`.
- **Primary hypothesis:** quota or admission rejection.
- **First evidence:** pod events, node allocatable `AcceleratorProfile custom resource`, operand readiness, recent changes, and `user impact incidents`.
- **Command path:** `chroot /host` followed by `oc get co`.

### Scenario 009: works after reboot but fails after upgrade

- **Context:** `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` in workload tier `4` on accelerator pool `gpu-pool-1`.
- **Primary hypothesis:** runtime injection or CDI failure.
- **First evidence:** pod events, node allocatable `AcceleratorProfile custom resource`, operand readiness, recent changes, and `failed profile mappings`.
- **Command path:** `lspci -nn | grep -Ei 'vga|3d|display|nvidia|amd|habana|ibm'` followed by `oc get resourcequota -A`.
- **Safe test:** run a minimal supported accelerator container on the same node without changing the production workload.
- **Remediation rule:** change one layer at a time and keep a tested rollback manifest or node-state recovery step.

### Scenario 010: capacity exists but scheduler reports insufficient resource

- **Context:** `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` in workload tier `5` on accelerator pool `gpu-pool-2`.
- **Primary hypothesis:** stale configuration after operator reconciliation.
- **First evidence:** pod events, node allocatable `AcceleratorProfile custom resource`, operand readiness, recent changes, and `migration completion`.
- **Command path:** `lsmod | grep -Ei 'nvidia|amdgpu|vfio|habana'` followed by `oc get pod <pod> -n <namespace> -o yaml`.
- **Safe test:** run a minimal supported accelerator container on the same node without changing the production workload.
- **Remediation rule:** change one layer at a time and keep a tested rollback manifest or node-state recovery step.
- **Validation:** prove device visibility, resource allocation, representative workload success, metrics recovery, and no regression on another node.
- **Prevention:** add a preflight check, drift alert, canary node, or admission control matching the discovered cause.
- **Escalation evidence:** must-gather, operator logs, node journal, hardware diagnostics, exact versions, timestamps, and reproducer.

### Scenario 011: metrics show utilization but application throughput is low

- **Context:** `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` in workload tier `1` on accelerator pool `gpu-pool-3`.
- **Primary hypothesis:** missing node label or toleration.

- **Validation:** prove device visibility, resource allocation, representative workload success, metrics recovery, and no regression on another node.
- **Prevention:** add a preflight check, drift alert, canary node, or admission control matching the discovered cause.
- **Escalation evidence:** must-gather, operator logs, node journal, hardware diagnostics, exact versions, timestamps, and reproducer.

### Scenario 130: capacity exists but scheduler reports insufficient resource

- **Context:** `Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge` in workload tier `5` on accelerator pool `gpu-pool-2`.
- **Primary hypothesis:** stale configuration after operator reconciliation.
- **First evidence:** pod events, node allocatable `AcceleratorProfile custom resource`, operand readiness, recent changes, and `migration completion`.
- **Command path:** `lsmod | grep -Ei 'nvidia|amdgpu|vfio|habana'` followed by `oc get pod <pod> -n <namespace> -o yaml`.
- **Safe test:** run a minimal supported accelerator container on the same node without changing the production workload.
- **Remediation rule:** change one layer at a time and keep a tested rollback manifest or node-state recovery step.
- **Validation:** prove device visibility, resource allocation, representative workload success, metrics recovery, and no regression on another node.
- **Prevention:** add a preflight check, drift alert, canary node, or admission control matching the discovered cause.
- **Escalation evidence:** must-gather, operator logs, node journal, hardware diagnostics, exact versions, timestamps, and reproducer.


## Production operating procedures

### SOP 01: New GPU node onboarding

1. Confirm approved change record, support matrix, maintenance window, owner, and rollback trigger.
2. Capture cluster, operator, node, driver, firmware, label, taint, quota, and workload before-state.
3. Drain or quiesce workloads according to disruption and data-protection requirements.
4. Apply the smallest approved change through GitOps or the supported operator interface.
5. Observe reconciliation, daemonsets, node readiness, allocatable resources, and vendor diagnostics.
6. Run minimal smoke, OpenShift AI workload, telemetry, and SLO validation.
7. Rollback when trigger conditions are met; do not continue stacking unverified changes.
8. Close with evidence, lessons learned, configuration drift check, and runbook update.

## Troubleshooting quick reference

| Symptom | First checks | Likely layer | Safe next action |
|---|---|---|---|
| No PCI device | lspci, firmware, hypervisor assignment | Hardware or virtualization | Correct assignment or firmware; do not reinstall operator first |
| Driver pod failing | daemonset logs, kernel, MCP, Secure Boot | Driver | Resolve exact build/load error |
| Allocatable is zero | device plugin, NVML/vendor API, health | Device plugin or driver | Run vendor smoke on host and inspect plugin logs |
| Pod Pending | events, quota, selectors, taints, capacity | Scheduling or admission | Fix only the reported predicate or quota |
| GPU absent in container | resource request, assigned node, CDI/hooks, libraries | Runtime | Run minimal container and compare mounts/devices |
| CUDA or framework error | driver compatibility, memory, Xid, image | Application or compatibility | Reproduce with supported base image |
| Low utilization | queue, batch, data feed, CPU, storage, topology | Application or system bottleneck | Correlate accelerator and service metrics |
| High temperature | power, clocks, fan, ambient, neighboring nodes | Hardware and facility | Reduce load safely and inspect cooling |
| Intermittent unhealthy device | Xid, ECC, PCIe, reset, thermal | Hardware or driver | Preserve logs and run vendor diagnostics |
| Works only on one node | version, labels, firmware, topology, images | Configuration drift | Diff node state before changing anything |

## Glossary

- **Accelerator:** A specialized compute device used to speed machine-learning, inference, training, or data-processing workloads.
- **Allocatable:** The quantity of a node resource available for pod scheduling after system reservations and health decisions.
- **CDI:** Container Device Interface, a specification for describing device injection into containers.
- **CUDA:** NVIDIA parallel-computing platform and programming model.
- **DCGM:** NVIDIA Data Center GPU Manager used for health and telemetry.
- **Device plugin:** A kubelet extension that discovers, advertises, allocates, and health-checks specialized devices.
- **Extended resource:** A vendor-qualified integer resource such as nvidia.com/gpu or amd.com/gpu.
- **GPU Feature Discovery:** NVIDIA component that uses NFD to create GPU-specific node labels.
- **Hardware profile:** OpenShift AI abstraction used to present governed compute and accelerator choices to users.
- **MIG:** NVIDIA Multi-Instance GPU, hardware partitioning on supported GPUs.
- **NFD:** Node Feature Discovery, a Kubernetes add-on that labels node hardware and system features.
- **NUMA:** Non-uniform memory access topology that affects CPU, memory, NIC, and GPU locality.
- **ROCm:** AMD software platform for GPU computing.
- **ServingRuntime:** KServe resource describing how a model server runs and which model formats it supports.
- **vGPU:** A virtual GPU profile exposed to a virtual machine through a hypervisor and vendor software stack.
- **Xid:** An NVIDIA driver error report written to the kernel or system log.

## Final L3 acceptance statement

A production implementation of **Accelerator profiles legacy knowledge** is complete only when the hardware-to-application path is proven, `AcceleratorProfile custom resource` is governed, a representative OpenShift AI workload succeeds, metrics and alerts work, rollback is rehearsed, and the exact combination is verified as supported.
